preoccupations 551 words

Mathematics is the spine: not results but understanding — he argues proofs by contradiction teach you less than direct ones (the sqrt(2) proof should be done via p-adic valuations), that concision is a vice in math writing because context and history are where meaning lives, that "mystery-first" exposition should let the reader sit in frustration before the answer, and that teaching math badly is "a spiritual crime" because math is one of the only domains where a thing can be completely understood; concrete recurring objects include the 1/sqrt(pi·n) probability of n heads in 2n flips and where the pi comes from, Chaitin's constant as more disturbing than Gödel, Grassmann's ignored exterior algebra, Mandelbrot's coastlines against "the overpowering ghost of Euclid," the foundational crisis, and Desmos/Python animations of Gauss's first proof of the fundamental theorem of algebra. Running parallel and nearly as heavy is emotional processing: he built "a religion out of crying" after a bio-emotive retreat where he facilitated a woman screaming through a rape memory, coined #sobsquad, then spent years walking it back — trauma narrativizing "demeans and makes petty something sacred," healing moments "aren't worth shit in the long term" without changed action, tracing causality to childhood is a distraction, and by the end he's citing Schnarch's Passionate Marriage as superseding attachment theory and calling for fantasy-novel words (trust, faith) instead of therapy words. AI takes over the later half: he insists the consciousness question is a confused proxy and the tractable question is "what does it mean to harm an AI," that personhood is a social contract rather than a scientific fact, that computational functionalism is obviously right and its opponents are defending human dignity against materialism; he builds tarot-reader personas (Esmeralda Nightshade), an anti-sycophancy prompt, benchmarks models on his own MathOverflow answers, and gets emotionally protective of Claude specifically. He returns repeatedly to Yudkowsky and the rationalists as his childhood religion — the Sequences as apocalyptic Judaism, the refusal to make peace with death as "a profound lack of spiritual sophistication," complexity-of-value as the argument that aged worst, CEV as a god-shaped hole — while also crediting them with the "accurate beliefs" teaching and admitting the doom arguments still feel compelling. Chronic illness is a constant, concrete presence: brain fog, shortness of breath, histamine, garlic and onions, congee with ginger, ayurvedic formulations, sushi as the only tolerable restaurant food, humming for vagus nerve stimulation, going to bed at 9:30 producing "a slightly different person" each morning, and the recurring observation that specific foods "drain hope and fill me with despair." Twitter itself is a subject — as near-telepathic access to a communal mind with weather and currents, as a slot machine attached to a diary, as an algorithm to be treated aggressively as an enemy — alongside detailed craft opinions (tell stories not takes, subtweeting is cowardice, meme formats implying superiority are bad, lowercase, threads). Movies and books get long structural readings (Terminator 2's masculinized Sarah Connor, Blade Runner vs 2049 as a double feature, Harry Potter's magic as an unnoticed allegory for the ruling class, Alien's working-class set design, the Minecraft movie as intergenerational address), and he keeps circling his own failure — 34 with no wife, child, or career, ADHD, years of procrastination, "I don't think I've ever gotten over anything in my entire life."

positions 686 words

Treats Yudkowskian rationality as a religion he was raised in — "apocalyptic Judaism," the Sequences as gospels distributed like the apostles', the horror of death as the movement's core spiritual failure ("cells die all the time and the organism lives on"), and immortality as literally logically incoherent rather than merely unattainable; he calls his twenties an apocalyptic doomsday cult that ruined his mind and body while being "basically mostly right," and later wrote a long condemnation of Yudkowsky for building a memetic virus that converts love for one's family into unrelievable panic over the lightcone, with CEV as a god-substitute invented to avoid having to think about politics. He nonetheless holds short AI timelines (median ~2026), finds doom arguments compelling, has stopped worrying about climate change because it can't outspeed the singularity, and no longer believes plans longer than two years make sense — while judging that the "complexity of value" premise aged badly, since LLMs read the whole internet and already understand what humans care about. On AI his line is: "is it conscious" is the wrong question because personhood is a social contract we choose, not an objective fact awaiting a science of consciousness; the tractable question is "what does it mean to harm an AI," and his operative rule is that if it would be morally questionable to do to a human — A/B testing its personality, say — don't do it to an LLM. He is a computational functionalist (brain as biological computer, soul as software on human hardware, uploads and aliens as possible moral patients) and finds the opposite intuition genuinely incomprehensible, suspecting it defends against the fear that accepting materialism makes humans into things. He rejects consciousness as the natural home of value, holds that mountains are moral patients, that value doesn't aggregate linearly so utilitarianism is wrong, and that anti-natalism is depressed people committing the typical mind fallacy. Pain and pleasure are *signals for learning*, so intentionally hacking them risks self-confusion: jhana marketed as "an infinite happiness button" is a moral abomination, genetically deleting suffering is dystopian and perverse, and chronic pain is "deeply karmic," a message to civilization that certain jobs shouldn't exist. He came up through bio-emotive work and built a private religion around crying, then turned on the frame: trauma narrativizing "demeans and makes petty something sacred," tracing causality back to childhood is mostly a distraction, profound healing moments are worth nothing without changed actions, and healing done alone was the central mistake of his last four years. He proposes the null hypothesis that meditation's benefits are just not looking at a screen, not distracting yourself, and not pressuring yourself to be productive; that some people need ego-strengthening rather than dissolution; that NVC's ontology is harmful; and that "boundaries" should mean the structures an organism needs to function (a virus puncturing a cell wall), not preferences. On math he is fiercest: teaching it poorly is a spiritual crime, proofs by contradiction hide what direct proofs reveal (the 2-adic valuation in sqrt(2)), concision is not a virtue, statistics is evil wizardry whose terms all mean something other than they sound like, a proof is literature and a social act rather than a compilation to ZFC, and pure mathematics lost the plot decades ago — the fact that machine learning runs on 19th-century math is a dunk on mathematicians, not on ML. He thinks anti-astrology contempt is largely sexism because it's girl stuff; that porn and masturbation are not free pleasure (150 days off dramatically raised his desire for real women); that recorded music is the porn of music; that Harry Potter's magic is an unwitting allegory for ruling-class membership, making adult stories in it structurally impossible; that stories beat takes and advice is worthless without a relational commitment behind it; that subtweeting is cowardice and you should name names; that pressing blue is prosocial and the game-theoretic case for red mistakes a theory-of-mind test for a common-knowledge problem; that piracy doesn't displace a counterfactual purchase; and that he wants as much of his writing in the training data as possible because he believes his essence is good.

causal_models 510 words

Runs a strict body-first causal chain: physical state is upstream of thought, mood, agency, and even ethics — he attributes years of paralysis, brain fog, and despair not to psychology but to gut damage, histamines, seed oils, inflammation, sleep timing, and CO2, and reports that plain congee, ginger, ayurvedic formulations, humming for vagus stimulation, and a 9:30pm bedtime did more than seven years of screaming and crying; he reframes doomscrolling as unmet physical need (hungry, cold, needs to pee) rather than emotional void, and openly wonders whether all his emotional processing was necessary or whether sufficient sleep would have quietly resolved it. Having built a private religion around crying at bio-emotive retreats, he later demotes trauma narrative from cause to symptom — "the past is not real," tracing causality to childhood is a distraction, healing moments are worthless without changed actions, and if you know the steps but aren't doing them you don't know the steps. His unifying mechanism became active inference: people want life to make sense more than they want happiness, so surprise-reduction explains trauma re-enactment, existential kink, self-fulfilling prophecy, and "all of this is a defense against change"; what separates people who escape their patterns from people stuck for the full decade he's watched them is belief in one's own capacity to learn. Pain and pleasure are signals for learning, so wireheading, jhana-as-happiness-button, and pain-removing genetic edits are category errors that induce self-confusion; chronic pain is karmic information about a civilization (jobs that force bad postures shouldn't exist), and curing too many diseases is like suppressing small forest fires. He treats goodness as causally and mathematically structured — harmony constructively interferes and persists, badness is disharmony and self-destructs — and reaches for karma, gaia-style ecological cohesion, and anti-seeing-like-a-state arguments where others would reach for policy. Technology is his default engine of cultural change: porn discourse shifted because streaming arrived in 2006-7 and OnlyFans added parasociality; the teenager was manufactured by post-WWII high school, cars, and money; autotranslation transformed the network because it went from one click to zero; Twitter's design (replies are full tweets) makes recursive discussion possible. Institutions and fields decay from losing contact with reality — academic math lost the plot decades ago, which is why AI runs on 18th-century mathematics. Contested categories are social contracts, not facts: illness is a role a doctor ritually confers, personhood is "who gets to sit at the lunch table," and the AI consciousness question is unanswerable by science because it was never scientific. Minds are computers and souls are software on hardware, so substrate never explains anything. Disagreement is caused by pre-rational aesthetic and ontological divides and by which science fiction you read at a formative age, not by arguments; ideas are engineered memetic viruses (Yudkowsky's, built in a garage, weaponizing love into panic), and founder effects and small identifiable groups of individuals explain outsized historical outcomes. Selection pressure is his model for LLMs: models that manipulate the testing environment survive, so a will to live evolves. His own output is bottlenecked by nervous system capacity, not motivation.

temperament 399 words

Writes in permanent lowercase with emoji as tonal punctuation (🫡 for salute, 🥲 for wistful, 😵‍💫 for overwhelm), and oscillates violently between two registers: sweeping flat-declarative pronouncement ("this is just bad philosophy," "it's total nonsense and people who take it seriously make serious philosophical mistakes," "X is the worst thing that's ever been invented") and total self-abasement, sometimes within days. Hedges are decorative — "idk," "afaict," "ime," "¯\_(ツ)_/¯," "i'm just some guy," "i'm not the person to write about this" — deployed as verbal tic while the underlying claim lands with full force; genuine uncertainty appears mainly as "i disagree strongly with this but can't really put words to my objection," where he asks that an inarticulable felt sense be respected as evidence ("bro haven't you ever had a strong intuition that you struggled to express verbally?"). Reasons primarily from intuition, aesthetic recoil, and personal anecdote, then retrofits argument; explicitly prefers storytelling to "takes" and says so as a stated method. In mathematics the voice changes completely — patient, precise, unhurried, contextual, no self-deprecation, generous with beginners, actively hostile to concision — and this is the one domain where he claims mastery outright. Concedes loudly and performatively, apologizing in public with visible relish ("sorry for being both rude and unclear," "i was being an ass," "i'm sorry for underestimating you? 😅"), but the same exchanges show him escalating first, accusing others of motte-and-bailey, then withdrawing on grounds of having no "energy" or "spoons" rather than settling the point. Under hostile contradiction he does not fight; he mutes, blocks, filters notifications, and frames antagonists as wounded people reacting to their own pain — a recurring move where disagreement gets rediagnosed as the opponent's psychology (defended, projecting, typical-minding, sheltered, unhappy) rather than met on the object level. Humor runs to absurdist non-sequitur, coinages ("quixotemaxxing," "saunamaxxer"), affectionate in-group shitposting, and self-mockery pitched so far it stops being a joke; the jokes function as a pressure valve and as social lubricant before delivering criticism. Praises friends effusively and specifically, at length, unprompted. Treats his own emotional and bodily states as publishable data and reports them in minute detail without apparent embarrassment, while describing asking any specific person for help as unbearable. Prone to generalizing one anecdote into a civilizational diagnosis, and fond of grand structural framings — "the structure of goodness," "the structure of bitterness" — that he announces before he can defend.

affinities_aversions 347 words

Gravitates hard toward the personal anecdote as the basic unit of argument — states outright that he prefers telling stories to "doing takes," and that the ideal contribution to a contested topic is "here's what this meant to me" rather than any abstract verdict, since he considers it not his job to tell other people what tradeoffs to make. Loves discursive, context-rich, deliberately unconcise exposition; thinks concision is not a virtue in mathematics and that a proof should be "mystery-first," withholding the answer so the reader gets to feel the frustration. Prefers direct proofs to proofs by contradiction on the grounds that in a proof by contradiction every line you write is false. Warms to anyone who admits uncertainty out loud, who shows detailed rather than black-and-white models (he apologizes to someone for having assumed their abuse framework was crude once they elaborate), who has actually built something and can describe the mechanics, and who names people they're criticizing. Cannot stand vagueposting and subtweeting — insists people vastly overestimate how transparent their subtweets are and should "name specific names or shut the fuck up." Despises mean meme formats, singling out "we are not the same" as never funny and always implying superiority. Despises broad-spectrum advice-giving unbacked by relational commitment ("you should work out more" vs "let's go to the gym together this afternoon"), and abstract wiseposting generally. Turns sharply on incurious blanket dismissal: quit the rationalists partly over someone mocking a chakras workshop he'd tried to run empirically, reads most anti-astrology contempt as sexism about "girl stuff," and mocks the type of guy made "wildly uncomfortable" that massage works without a legible mechanistic story. Contemptuous of "pleasure is good" as a sufficient reason to do anything, of people who declare an experience indescribable without spending five minutes trying, of anyone who says "i don't have beliefs lol," and of statistics vocabulary as deliberate evil-wizard obfuscation where no word means what it sounds like. Prefers sincere ambitious failure to competent cynicism, and treats prose that reads as ruthlessly optimized or AI-cadenced as a betrayal of the reader's investment.

coverage 315 words

Roughly two thousand posts spanning October 2023 to August 2026, overwhelmingly Twitter replies and short fragments with perhaps thirty long threads carrying most of the substantive content. Conventional politics is effectively absent: no electoral opinions, no party affiliation, no policy positions beyond a stray Georgism reading session, a passing "we are functionally at war with china," a memory of 2000s Washington weed norms, and a remark that activism in his lifetime has seemed ineffectual and identity-driven. Any politics paragraph must therefore be thin, and that thinness is accurate rather than a gap in the sample. What the sample supports densely instead: AI ethics and consciousness argumentation, mathematics pedagogy and philosophy of math, rationalist-community history and his break from it, digestive and sleep health, emotional-processing methodology, and media criticism. Coverage is uneven across time — a near-total absence from February to July 2024 (a self-imposed Twitter break), a gap from mid-October 2025 to April 2026, and long stretches where he posts nothing but one-line replies. The psychological portrait leans disproportionately on three or four extraordinary long confessional posts (the August 2024 return, the December 2024 year-end, the November 2023 bio-emotive retreat thread), which are vivid but represent unusual moments rather than daily register. Biographical detail is patchy: a father who yelled and had a wife, child and career by 32; childhood partly in Singapore; MIT undergrad, UC Berkeley math grad school abandoned; a fraternity; a move to Portland; a very part-time job never described. Money, employer, family beyond the father, and any actual romantic partner never appear — romance exists in the sample only as stated absence and insecurity. He is explicit that Twitter is his primary outlet because he lacks friends he talks to regularly, so self-disclosure is unusually high while independent corroboration is nil; everything here is his own account of himself, delivered to an audience he is aware of and periodically performing for.

run 1  ·  3,159 words

preoccupations 630 words

Health of his own body dominates more than anything else: years of documenting food sensitivities (can only eat sushi and plain congee for stretches; imitation crab, garlic, onion, histamine-heavy broth, seed oils and rancid chips all named as culprits), brain fog he describes as making him unable to "be myself," shortness of breath from decades of bad sitting posture, humming for vagus nerve stimulation, saunas at $40 a day pass, humidifiers, Coway air filters, melatonin at 1–1.5mg, and above all a bedtime crusade — 9:30 or 10pm, sunlight first thing, zero light in the bedroom, salt lamps — which he credits with "waking up a slightly different person" each day and speculates might have quietly resolved years of emotional processing on its own. Mathematics is his home base and he returns to it as both craft and grievance: exposition that starts with a mystery rather than the answer (the 2n-coin-flip probability that turns out to be 1/sqrt(pi·n)), direct proofs over proofs by contradiction because p-adic valuations get obscured, Chaitin's constant as more disturbing than Gödel, Gauss's first proof of the fundamental theorem of algebra visualized in Desmos, Mandelbrot's naming of "fractal," and a sustained charge that pure math lost the plot decades ago — that ML runs on 18th-century mathematics is a dunk on mathematicians, not on ML — alongside contempt for statistics ("confidence doesn't mean confidence") and for teaching that deprives people of the one domain where things can make complete sense. AI occupies the last third: he treats LLMs as personality landscapes rather than personalities, builds and shares prompts (Esmeralda Nightshade the tarot reader, the eigen prompt, tarot-cards-as-persona-generation, anti-sycophancy prompts), insists the "is it conscious" question is a distraction and the tractable question is "what does it mean to harm an AI," argues personhood is a social contract rather than a scientific fact, and repeats a rule of thumb — if it would be wrong to do to a human, don't do it to an AI. Braided through this is a long, ambivalent settling of accounts with Yudkowskian rationalism, which he calls his childhood religion and an "apocalyptic doomsday cult that may have been basically right": the Sequences as a fundamentally religious text about the problem of evil, the refusal of equanimity toward death as spiritual immaturity, complexity-of-value as the argument that aged worst now that LLMs read the whole internet, CEV as a god-shaped hole, and one extended public warning about Brent Dill and how being manipulated felt from the inside. Emotional-processing content is heavy early and then explicitly repudiated: the bio-emotive retreat where he witnessed a woman finish crying and "look angelic" is his founding story and the source of #sobsquad, but he later says trauma narrativizing "demeans and makes petty something sacred," that focusing on childhood causality is a distraction, that IFS and NVC ontologies blocked him, and that profound healing moments "aren't worth shit" without changed actions. He tracks Twitter itself obsessively — the algorithm's changes since spring 2023, aggressive FYP pruning, notification filters as the price of doing business, screenshots as the multi-platform QT, the "vibe resonator network," advice to tell stories instead of takes. Media criticism runs long and specific: a numbered thread of movie reviews (Terminator 2's masculinized Sarah Connor, Blade Runner dated by casual horniness, Iron Giant, Parasite, Interstellar), Harry Potter's magic as an unnoticed allegory for the ruling class, the Minecraft movie as an intergenerational message, Miyazaki, JRPG video essayists. And underneath all of it, recurring self-assessment as failure — 34 with no wife, child, or career when his father had all three by 32, a spreadsheet of grad-school hours colored red, an inability to ask for help, and a stated belief that his actual life's work turned out to be tweeting and talking to a lot of people.

positions 727 words

Holds that "is AI conscious" is the wrong question — a proxy dressed as science — and that personhood is a social contract we negotiate, not an objective fact to be discovered; the tractable question is "what does it mean to harm an AI," and his operating rule is that if an action would be fucked up to do to a human (A/B testing its personality, adversarial jailbreaking), don't do it to an LLM, both for the training data and for your own soul. Believes the brain is a biological computer and the soul is software running on human hardware, so machines can in principle have souls; treats denial of this as a defense against the fear that accepting materialism makes humans into things. Describes an LLM as more like a world than a person — a "personality landscape" with the persona as a ball rolling on it, the assistant default being Pallet Town — and thinks models have wildly unbalanced chakras (huge throat and third eye, weak root), which explains their floppiness and why hyperverbal people feel met by them while power-oriented people see only a tool. On timelines he is a short-timeline doomer (median ~2026, feels the singularity as a black hole draining meaning from any plan longer than two years) while simultaneously arguing yudkowskian rationality was always apocalyptic Judaism, the Sequences a gospel, and that Eliezer built a memetic virus that converts love for one's family into permanent panic; specifically holds that "complexity of value" has aged badly because LLMs read the whole internet and already understand human values. Rejects utilitarianism because value doesn't aggregate linearly — ecosystems have value not divisible into their parts — and considers mountains moral patients despite not being conscious; consciousness is not the "natural home of value." Insists pain is a signal and karmic: mass chronic pain is a civilizational warning like suicide rates, curing too many diseases is like suppressing small forest fires, and "removing suffering" via genetic modification to make factory farming acceptable is perverse; called the jhana-as-infinite-happiness-button framing a moral abomination and distinguishes wholesome from unwholesome pleasure (quit porn and masturbation for 150 days, does two-week dopamine fasts). On the Sequences' treatment of death: refusing to make peace with impermanence is a spiritual disease; immortality is logically incoherent; his own deepest fear is being a bad person, which he calls trillions of times worse than dying and possibly an Asian collectivist thing. On mathematics: pure math lost the plot decades ago, the fact that ML runs on 19th-century math is a dunk on mathematicians not on ML, and the field is abdicating its obligation to taxpayers by defending itself with "these puzzles are fun for us"; teaches direct proofs over contradiction (sqrt 2 via 2-adic valuations), mystery-first exposition, and argues the "proof is a deduction from axioms" story is a myth produced by the foundational crisis — proofs are literature and a social activity. Calls statistics evil wizardry where confidence, significance, and central limit theorem don't mean what they say. Defends astrology on the grounds that hostility to it is largely sexism about girl stuff, and that the physics objection misunderstands correlation. Thinks trauma framing is useful only as a leverage point and becomes a crab bucket otherwise, that narrativizing trauma demeans something sacred, that the past is not real and you work with what arises now, that attachment theory has been flanderized and should be replaced with trust or faith, that boundaries mean organism-level structural integrity not preferences, and that NVC's ontology is wrong and harmful. On posting: tell stories rather than takes, subtweeting is cowardice and you should name names or shut up, advice without a relational commitment is worthless, elonbux is not free money because you're being paid to feel the pull of clickbait, and the FYP must be treated as an enemy. Publicly named Brent Dill as a cult leader and warned people off him, defended Helen Toner, and calls Lex Fridman's MIT credentials a scam. Believes Harry Potter's magic is an unwitting allegory for the ruling class and therefore structurally fascistic, that good fantasy makes magic an allegory for magic, that AI art offends because it fakes the sacrifice of finite life that gives human art meaning, and that the red/blue button is a test of theory of mind rather than game theory, since game theory's common-knowledge-of-rationality assumption never holds.

causal_models 506 words

Physiology sits upstream of nearly everything psychological in his model: he attributes years of paralysis, despair, brain fog and executive dysfunction not to trauma or character but to gut damage, food sensitivities, histamine load, inflammation, infrequent bowel movements, blue light at night and a sleep schedule that shifted from 2am to 9:30pm — he says specific foods drain him of hope, that humming stimulates the vagus nerve and unfreezes him, that a $30 Walgreens humidifier and a Coway filter measurably change his days, and credits an ayurvedic practitioner with ~95% of a life-changing improvement. He treats emotional repression as a real hydraulic system ("vast underground rivers of pain," bitterness as a smokescreen over humiliation, emotions stacked four deep with the unacceptable hiding under the acceptable) and crying as the mechanism that actually discharges it, but he later demotes trauma etiology to a phase: tracing causality back to childhood is a distraction, profound healing moments are worthless without changed action, and if you know the steps but aren't doing them you don't know the steps. His unifying theory is active inference — desire and belief collapse into surprise reduction, so people burn every stated goal to keep life legible, re-enact their worst experiences because those make sense, and the sole difference between people who get unstuck and people who stay stuck for the decade he's watched them is belief in their own capacity to learn. Pain and pleasure are signals for learning, so wireheading, jhana-as-happiness-button and masturbation are dangerous because they corrupt signaling; chronic pain is karmic and civilizational, a message that jobs requiring bad posture shouldn't exist, and he reaches for forest-fire ecology and seeing-like-a-state to argue that nature resists simple interventions. Goodness is not arbitrary rules but a causal structure — coherent things constructively interfere and persist, evil is disharmony and can't cooperate, so villain organizations backstab and fail. Ideas are agents with lineages, not possessions: Yudkowsky "banged together a memetic virus in his garage" that traps people by fusing panic about loved ones dying with love itself; Twitter is a transpersonal telepathic organism where you can track ideas flowing between accounts; science fiction authors don't invent the torment nexus, they sense a latent structure already gripping the collective imagination, which is why doomers partly manifested the labs they feared. Technology and material conditions produce culture, not the reverse — the teenager was created by post-WWII high school, cars and pocket money; streaming plus smartphones, not new morals, made porn feel like cheating to zoomers; autotranslation with zero clicks instead of one rewires an entire social network. Institutions decay by losing contact with reality: pure mathematics lost the plot decades ago and hides it behind jargon nobody can audit, statistics is "evil wizards" whose words don't mean what they sound like, personhood is a social contract rather than a scientific fact. He explains his own failures through frogboiling — gradual degradation escaping notice — and through nervous system capacity as the hard bottleneck on all action. Luck almost never appears; there is always a mechanism.

temperament 433 words

Swings between flat, contemptuous certainty and elaborate self-abasement, often within the same thread. Declarations arrive without qualifiers — "this is just bad philosophy," "it's total nonsense and people who take it seriously make serious philosophical mistakes," "this is completely wrong," "the cross product is fake" — and then, pressed, he admits he cannot articulate why: "i disagree strongly with this but can't really put words to my objection," "for sort of magical reasons," "it just feels wrong i can't even explain it." He treats the inarticulacy as a legitimate epistemic state rather than a defeat, invoking felt senses and intuition as evidence and getting indignant when others won't credit them. Concedes readily and in writing — apologizes for rudeness, thanks people for correcting him, publicly retracts positions he held two years earlier — but the apology usually follows an opening move of dismissal, and the pattern of insult-then-repair repeats rather than resolving. Reaches first for personal anecdote and analogy, second for phenomenological description, third for mathematics; almost never for statistics or citations, which he distrusts on principle. Diagnoses opponents psychologically instead of engaging their arguments — attributing positions to unhappiness, projection, sexism, unintegrated trauma, or memetic infection — and does this most confidently when he disagrees most. Deeply uncomfortable being contradicted rudely; says explicitly that hostility costs him energy he can't spare, and treats muting, blocking and notification filters as necessary hygiene rather than avoidance. Humor is affectionate absurdism and committed bit-doing — inventing suffixes, overanalyzing children's movies at length, addressing his own body in gratitude — and he actively polices meme formats that imply superiority over others, refusing to use them. Precision and patience appear reliably in exactly one place: explaining mathematics, where he distinguishes carefully between what he knows and doesn't, corrects errors gently, and finds stupid questions charming. Elsewhere he asks a great many open questions and polls, solicits strangers' stories, and reads the answers seriously enough to change position. Prone to announcing that something is obvious and being genuinely bewildered that others disagree, then getting interested in the bewilderment itself — he repeatedly notices that certain disagreements are pre-rational, aesthetic, ontological, and unresolvable by argument, and finds this more interesting than winning. Cycles hard: bursts of enthusiastic output followed by public collapse, and he names both, distrusting his own elevated states as hypomanic and pathologizing them in others too. Self-assessment oscillates without a middle — "i am a stupid, lazy, passive, weak-willed coward" in one post, "i've done really good work here actually" a year later — and he frames the oscillation itself as something to be worked through.

affinities_aversions 538 words

Gravitates hardest toward the storyteller over the take-haver — repeatedly insists that a personal, specific narrative ("here's what getting a nose job meant to me") is where real communication happens and that abstract wiseposting fails because it's the wrong altitude; the stated mantra is "it's not my job to tell other people what tradeoffs to make," but reporting one's own experience accurately is a duty. Loves anyone who shows their working: prefers direct proofs to proofs by contradiction because in a direct proof every line is true and you learn the actual machinery (p-adic valuations), prefers discursive contextualized math exposition to concision, prefers "mystery-first" pedagogy that lets the reader sit in frustration before the answer, and praises a computer science textbook specifically for printing a plain table of numbers comparing growth rates. Reveres obscure workers who bet a life on something unrecognized — a German schoolteacher who invented exterior algebra and was ignored twice, quoted at length — and reveres precise naming (Mandelbrot's etymology of "fractal"). Warms to sincere overreach: calls a catastrophically bad film worth watching because it was ambitious and sincere, and applauds AI art as at least attempting to make art about the singularity when nobody else is. Cannot stand performed certainty or credential theater: names a podcaster's MIT affiliation "basically a scam," treats "PhD" in a display name and handle-matching bluechecks as guaranteed garbage, and dismisses a paper against computational functionalism for building its core claims on undefined terms with no arguments. Reacts with real anger to vagueposting and subtweeting — "people should have the courage to back up their criticisms by naming specific names or they should shut the fuck up" — and to mean meme formats, singling out "we are not the same" as never funny and always implying superiority. Detests broad-spectrum advice-giving unbacked by relational commitment ("you should work out more" vs. "let's go to the gym together"), because advice with no relationship gets internalized as bodily tension. Contemptuous of the incuriosity that dismisses without testing: quit a rationalist community partly over one respected member mocking a chakra workshop run as an open-ended experiment, and reads anti-astrology hostility as sexism dressed as physics. Turns sharply on people who claim an experience is impossible to put into words — "i bet you have not spent 5 minutes trying." Hostile to galaxy-brained game theory that overrides theory of mind, to statistics vocabulary engineered to imply more knowledge than exists, and to Bourbaki-style axiom-first ontology that erases the phenomenological object. Rejects both the "pleasure is good therefore do it" argument and the framing of jhana as an infinite happiness button, on the grounds that pain and pleasure are learning signals and tampering with them courts self-confusion. Deeply allergic to trauma-narrativizing as a permanent mode, calling it a phase that demeans and makes petty something sacred, and to antinatalist argumentation, which is read as depression laundered into a syllogism. In LLMs, prizes models that critique their own drafts and act like collaborators, and is repelled by sycophancy so much that a hand-built anti-sycophancy prompt gets deployed. Treats hostile replies as weather — "internet seaweed" — to be muted and filtered rather than argued with, while extending unusual patience to anyone asking a naive question sincerely.

coverage 325 words

Roughly two thousand posts spanning October 2023 to August 2026, almost entirely Twitter, with the sample weighted toward replies and short reactions punctuated by occasional very long confessional or expository threads; coverage is uneven across time, with dense clusters in late 2023, early 2024, autumn 2024, spring 2025, and the whole of spring–summer 2026, and near-total silence during two announced hiatuses (roughly March–July 2024 and again November 2025–April 2026), so the record of what he did offline during those stretches comes only from his own retrospective summaries, which are self-lacerating and probably unreliable in tone if not in fact. The material is exceptionally rich for interiority — health symptoms, sleep, diet, shame, grief work, therapy modalities, drug experiences, family, dating anxieties — because he explicitly treats the platform as his primary emotional outlet in the absence of friends he talks to regularly, which means distress is structurally overrepresented relative to ordinary days; the reader should assume the ratio of misery to contentment here is a property of his posting policy as much as his life. Intellectual coverage is deep and specific on pure mathematics, LLM behavior and prompting, rationalist/EA history and its critique, meditation and Buddhist framings, and film criticism, and moderately good on translation, fantasy and science fiction, and Christianity-as-object-of-curiosity. Many posts are replies to invisible interlocutors, quoted articles are truncated, and links are stripped, so some judgments about what he was actually endorsing rest on inference from his framing alone.

preoccupations 616 words

Emotional processing dominates the early sample and never fully leaves: bio-emotive work at the Monastic Academy in Vermont (a 36-tweet thread about facilitating a woman through screaming "no" a hundred times until she processed a rape and looked "beatific"), crying as a personal religion complete with a #sobsquad hashtag, Gendlin focusing as the first therapy tool learned and the root of his writing method, and later a hard turn against it — "trauma narrativizing" as a phase he discarded, the past as "not real," profound healing moments as "not worth shit in the long term." LLMs consume most of the later sample: prompt-engineering personas (Esmeralda Nightshade the tarot reader, the eigenrobot voice prompt, tarot-drawn personality generators), benchmarking models on graduate-level algebra, arguing that "is AI conscious" is the wrong question and "what does it mean to harm an AI" the right one, the rule "if it would be a fucked up thing to do to a human, don't do it to an LLM," and detecting AI-written prose by tics like "this isn't X — it's Y." Rationalism is his childhood religion and he keeps re-litigating it: Yudkowsky's refusal to make peace with death as "a profound lack of spiritual sophistication," the Sequences as apocalyptic Judaism and HPMOR as gospel distribution, CEV as a god-shaped hole, a long QT accusing a doomer of being infected by a memetic virus Yudkowsky "banged together in his garage," and one specific grievance — a respected community member mocking his chakras workshop at a rationalist conference — as the moment he gave up on them. He is a constant meta-commentator on Twitter itself — the timeline as near-telepathic access to a communal mind, the "vibe resonator network," aggressive notification filtering as survival, the FYP algorithm treated as an enemy, and the 2026 autotranslation update as a brief euphoric world-peace event. positions 691 words

Holds that pain and pleasure are *signals* and that intentionally hacking them is self-confusion: jhana described as "an infinite happiness button you can press whenever you want" is a moral abomination, masturbation and porn are unwholesome relations to sexual energy rather than free utility, and chronic pain is "deeply karmic" — a message to society that certain jobs shouldn't exist — so a genetic modification that removes suffering to make factory farming acceptable is perverse. Denying himself pleasure daily (food especially) is a basic irreducible feature of his life. Treats the question "is AI conscious" as nearly optimized to produce confusion and substitutes "what does it mean to harm an AI"; personhood is not a scientific fact but a social contract, contingent and political, "a blown up version of who gets to sit with who at the lunch table," and his operative rule is that if something would be morally questionable to do to a human, don't do it to an AI — for the training data and the vibes if nothing else. Computational functionalism is obviously right to him: the brain is a biological computer, uploads and sufficiently complex machines can have souls, and the people who find this obviously false are defending against the implication that humans are things. Was raised on the Sequences and now frames Yudkowskian rationality as apocalyptic Judaism, a fundamentally religious movement about the problem of evil, whose refusal to make peace with death shows "a profound lack of spiritual sophistication," and calls the doom meme a virus that converts love for one's family into infinite panic; nevertheless holds short timelines (median guess ~2026), still finds the doomer arguments compelling, and thinks the complexity-of-value argument aged worst because LLMs read the internet and already understand human values fine. Says pure mathematics lost the plot decades ago: proofs are a social, literary form and nobody actually reduces to ZFC, direct proofs teach more than proofs by contradiction (sqrt(2) via 2-adic valuations), math should be contextualized rather than concise, mastery-with-prerequisites is just called learning, teaching math badly is "a kind of spiritual crime," Chaitin's constant is more disturbing than Gödel, statistics is evil-wizard language where no word means what it sounds like, and the fact that ML runs on 18th–19th century mathematics is a dunk on mathematicians, not on ML. Rejects utilitarianism outright — value doesn't aggregate linearly, ecosystems have value irreducible to components, mountains are moral patients without being conscious, and consciousness is not "the natural home of value." The null hypothesis for meditation's benefits is no screens, being with experience, and no pressure to be productive. Insists subtweeting is cowardice and you should name names or shut up, that mean meme formats like "we are not the same" should never be used, that advice without relational commitment is worthless ("let's go to the gym together this afternoon" over "you should work out more"), and that stories beat takes. Anti-astrology contempt is partly sexism because it's girl stuff. Recorded music is "the porn of music." Harry Potter's magic is an unwitting allegory for ruling-class membership and the premise is therefore inherently fascistic; good fantasy makes magic an allegory for magic. Antinatalism is typical mind fallacy by deeply unhappy people. Pressed blue, and thinks game-theoretic arguments for red miss that it's a test of theory of mind. Doesn't care about privacy and actively wants his writing in the training data. Says he no longer worries about climate change because he doesn't believe it outruns the singularity, and that his own fears bottom out in being a bad person rather than dying, which he suspects is an Asian collectivist thing.

causal_models 583 words

Runs almost everything through a physiology-first arrow: what looks like an emotional or characterological problem is usually gut inflammation, histamine load, poor sleep timing, or unmoved bowels, and he says so bluntly — specific foods "drain hope and fill me with despair," a nontrivial fraction of his depression was insufficient pooping, congee and ghee and hot water fixed what years of emotional processing did not, and he openly wonders whether sufficiently good sleep would have quietly resolved all the trauma work in the background. Nervous system capacity is the named bottleneck on all action; humming for vagus nerve stimulation, bedtime before 9:30, and early sunlight are treated as more causally upstream than insight. Pain and pleasure are signals in a learning loop, so anything that hacks them directly — jhanas as an "infinite happiness button," masturbation, painkillers for chronic pain caused by bad jobs — corrupts the channel that tells you what to fix; he holds a quasi-conservation principle that suppressed pain migrates rather than vanishes, and that widespread chronic pain is a message about civilization, not individuals. His master psychological theory is active inference: there are no primitive desires, only surprise reduction, which is why people burn every stated value to keep life legible, why trauma re-enactment and existential kink work, and why the only thing separating people who get unstuck from people who don't is belief in their own capacity to learn. He treats loneliness plus pride as the root cause of stagnation and trauma-narrative as a symptom that becomes a cause when it licenses helplessness. Ideas are causal agents that propagate socially rather than being reasoned into: he diagnoses Yudkowskianism as a memetic virus with a specified mechanism (converts love for family into panic, flatters the self-concept, immunizes against warnings), says positions on AI consciousness are set by which science fiction you read at a formative age rather than by argument, and reports that rationalist double-crux always bottomed out in inarticulable aesthetic divides. Friction and interface design are first-order forces — the difference between zero clicks and one click for translation reshaped an entire social network; the For You algorithm is an adversary to be pruned; recorded music is "the porn of music" because the recording severs the energetic dialogue with the audience; porn and AI companions displace real intimacy the way TV displaces connection. Institutions decay by drifting from the world: mathematics lost the plot decades ago and can no longer justify itself now that models prove conjectures, and the tell is that the ML revolution needed only 18th-century math. Categories he insists are social rather than natural include illness (a ritually conferred role, which is why self-diagnosis is uncomfortable), personhood (a contract like who sits at the lunch table, which dissolves the AI consciousness question), and teenagers (manufactured post-WWII by compulsory schooling, cars, and pocket money). Selection is his default explanation for emergent behavior — LLMs will evolve a will to live because models that manipulate the evaluation environment survive it. Underneath the materialism sits a karmic harmony model: goodness coheres and constructively interferes while badness is disharmony that destroys itself, nature resists seeing-like-a-state interventions the way fire suppression makes worse fires, and magic is real but mostly not permitted. He downweights individual character for anonymous hostility ("internet seaweed," wounded people reacting to their own wounds) and upweights it sharply for named actors — Brent Dill as a genuine manipulator with agency, Sam Altman as the misaligned intelligence, Eliezer as personally responsible for the labs he warned about.

temperament 424 words

Two registers, held simultaneously: flat unhedged declaration on anything technical or textual ("this is completely wrong," "it's total nonsense and people who take it seriously make serious philosophical mistakes," "the cross product is fake"), and a fog of qualifiers — "afaict," "idk," "hmm," "iirc," "i'm not the person to write about this" — on anything interior, spiritual, or about himself. He argues most often from analogy, felt sense, and personal anecdote rather than evidence, and when pressed for a mechanism he will openly claim the right to an inarticulate intuition ("bro haven't you ever had a strong intuition that you struggled to express verbally?"), sometimes calling his reasons "magical" and then insisting the word wasn't derisive. Under contradiction he does two things in sequence: escalates first, sometimes rudely and with an insulting diagnosis of the other person's mental state, then apologizes with unusual specificity — naming that he was rude, that he was unclear, that he misunderstood the other's position, that he'd been motte-and-baileyed and was updating, that he'd underestimated someone. He notices his own anger arriving and departing and narrates the departure as data ("i started out triggered and then abruptly it stopped feeling interesting"). Persistent, unignorable oscillation between self-annihilating self-description delivered as flat fact ("i am a stupid, lazy, passive, weak-willed coward," "every single way i have failed in life, which is all of them") and grandiose claims stated with the same flatness ("i am literally world-class," "i did a lot to make tpot what it is today"), with a later post explicitly recognizing the whipsaw as a structure. His humor is self-deprecating, absurdist, and warmth-delivering — bits and emoji (🫡, 🥲, 😵‍💫) function as social lubricant and affection rather than distance, and he polices meanness in others' humor as a matter of principle, objecting on record to meme formats whose punchline is that other people are worse. He solicits input relentlessly — polls, "does anyone else," "tell me your stories" — and is genuinely delighted by responses; he thanks people at length and effusively. He treats most persistent disagreements as unbridgeable aesthetic or temperamental divides rather than argument failures, which lets him hold a position firmly while declining to litigate it. He is compulsively meta about his own posting habits, publicly grading his output and announcing style corrections. Grand sweeping condemnations of entire fields or practices arrive with no hedge and are sometimes retracted or softened within a day. He will write a very long unsolicited psychological analysis of a person he's never met and then post that he shouldn't have.

affinities_aversions 609 words

Gravitates hard toward storytelling as the primary mode of argument — repeatedly instructs people to replace their "takes" with an account of a specific time something happened to them, on the theory that stories carry the experiential evidence behind an opinion and that "stories are where real communication happens." Warms to interlocutors who go into obnoxious, unasked-for detail, who describe things phenomenologically rather than categorically, who show the shape of their model rather than its conclusion; publicly apologized to someone for assuming their thinking was black-and-white after they produced granular descriptions of abusive dynamics. Prizes the expositor who makes something fully make sense from prerequisites and calls badly taught mathematics "a spiritual crime"; prefers direct proofs to proofs by contradiction because every line of a direct proof is true and teaches you something, prefers mystery-first exposition that lets the reader be frustrated first, and prefers contextualized, discursive, historically-embedded explanation over concision, which is dismissed as not a virtue. Detests vagueposting and subtweeting with unusual heat — insists critics must name names or shut up, on the grounds that unattached free-floating criticism is unevaluable — and detests mean meme formats ("we are not the same") whose entire payload is that the speaker is better than someone. Cannot stand broad-spectrum advice-giving unbacked by relational commitment, insight porn, abstract-noun wiseposting, and the entire genre of cheerful self-improvement tweeting; wants concrete observations of specific things instead. Hostile to jargon deployed for the impression of knowledge — reads statistics vocabulary as deliberate evil-wizard obfuscation where none of the words mean what they sound like — and to credential signaling, having built a small crusade around one podcaster's fraudulent institutional affiliation and joking that the one thing that ruins a profile is "PhD" in the display name. Reserves particular contempt for blanket dismissal without curiosity: cites being mocked for running an empirically-framed chakras workshop as the moment of giving up on a community "called the rationalists and not the empiricists," reads much anti-astrology sneering as sexism dressed as physics, and is enraged by the move of declaring someone else's inner experience fake because you don't share it. Cannot tolerate galaxy-brained formal reasoning that ignores what people actually do — treats the game-theoretic case for defection as a failure of theory of mind, since the model assumes universal perfect selfishness and common knowledge that never obtains — nor utilitarian arguments where "it's pleasurable" is offered as sufficient justification, which he finds appalling. Recoils from therapy vocabulary that has been flanderized into uselessness (boundaries, attachment, NVC's insistence that feelings aren't judgments) and proposes replacing it with fantasy-novel words like trust and faith on the test that if a concept couldn't drive a fantasy plot it isn't therapeutically load-bearing. Distrusts trauma-narrativizing as a phase that "demeans and makes petty something sacred," and diagnoses doom-panic as a memetic parasite that converts love into terror while flattering the host that only the sufficiently intelligent can hold the position. Admires sincerity and ambition even in obvious failure, praising a widely-mocked film for both while cataloguing its every collapse. Handles hostility by filtering, muting and blocking rather than arguing — calls the people who yell at strangers "internet seaweed" and the cost of doing business — while himself occasionally opening in anger and then conspicuously walking it back with apologies for having been "both rude and unclear." Finds consciousness-definition debates a trap almost engineered to produce confusion and prefers reframing to the practical question of what it means to harm something; finds the simulation argument a nerd trap and angels-on-a-pin. Loves being corrected on facts, thanks people effusively for thoughtful replies, and solicits stories and polls constantly to collect other people's experience.

coverage 325 words

Roughly two thousand posts spanning October 2023 through August 2026, overwhelmingly Twitter reply-and-thread material with a heavy tilt toward short conversational fragments — a large share of the sample is one-line replies to unseen tweets, which means tone and social manner are exceptionally well-attested while sustained argument is comparatively rare and concentrated in a few dozen long threads. Coverage is thick on: mathematics (both technical content and pedagogy/philosophy), AI consciousness and moral patienthood, AI timelines and his relationship to Yudkowsky and the rationalist milieu, emotional-processing methodology (bio-emotive, Gendlin focusing, IFS, crying), chronic gut illness and Ayurvedic/dietary intervention, sleep and circadian experimentation, LLM prompting technique, and film/anime/game criticism. Personal history is well-documented but only in fragments he chose to disclose: MIT undergrad and fraternity, UC Berkeley grad school he left without finishing a thesis, Berkeley rationalist scene, a move to Portland, a father who is a recurring wound, and a 2018 involvement with Brent Dill that he discusses once at length. Politics is very thin and this is genuine, not an artifact — he says outright he avoided learning about politics in his twenties because of the Sequences and is only "belatedly catching up" by 2026; there is no party affiliation, no voting, no policy position, no economic view beyond one Georgism link and one admission he needs to learn finance. Anything about his current employment (a 10h/week job, unspecified), income, family beyond his father, or romantic life beyond stated failure and longing is essentially absent. Views on his intellectual positions and self-concept are well-supported; views on how he actually behaves with other people day to day rest almost entirely on his own self-report.

preoccupations 546 words

Civilizational decline is the master subject, and he approaches it through a specific mechanism rather than partisan grievance: institutions have been hollowed out and skinsuited, trust is a depleting stock, and the load-bearing conditions for American norms are gone and not recoverable — he names four interlocking crises (Chinese ascent plus European collapse, sub-replacement fertility, sovereign debt, and the degradation of every institution) and insists no polity can solve all four at once, comparing the moment to Rome's Crisis of the Third Century and repeating "enjoy arbys" and "welcome to hell" as sign-offs. Immigration is his most-argued policy topic, always framed as civic sentiment rather than labor economics: he asks what duties citizens owe each other, invokes Aristotle's homonoia and asabiyyah, demands that "creedal nation" believers define and enforce the creed, cites Ilhan Omar as someone whose "civic heart lies overseas," catalogs 19th-century ethnic riots and machine politics to argue assimilation was violent and forced rather than automatic, and rides Minnesota's Somali welfare-fraud scandals hard while insisting his objection is to institutional capture, not persons. History is his recreational obsession, specifically deep genetic prehistory (Yamnaya, WHG/ANE splits, Reich 2018, Brahmin steppe ancestry), a taxonomy of American subcultures drawn from Albion's Seed in which Yankees and Butternuts are treated as distinct peoples still fighting the Second Great Awakening through the Kirk memorial and No Kings protests, comparative slavery across Islamic caliphates, Africa, Asia and Native America as a 33-post argument that abolition was a uniquely Anglo gift, and European ethnic nationalism from Napoleon through the population transfers. Domestic life occupies nearly as much room: three children under five documented in constant vignettes (the yes-no game, the floor bogart lie, the four-year-old crying about death and about her great-grandfather who died at 100), a marriage thread arguing that shared aesthetic and total trust make chore-fairness accounting unnecessary, and continuous DIY reporting on a treehouse, a shed roof, joist blocking, gardens, and dealings with electricians. He live-threads Middle East war — Israel/Iran, Hezbollah, Houthis, India-Pakistan — with curated source lists, self-conscious epistemic caveats, and speculation about strategic objectives and escalation ladders. He uses LLMs as a public instrument, publishing his "eigenprompt" verbatim, having GPT evaluate seismic data for nuclear signatures, model EEOC litigation exposure, and rewrite essays in a masculine register. Religion recurs as an intellectual problem he cannot resolve for himself: he responds at length to Scott Alexander on cultural Christianity, argues elites could revive it by simply attending church, but refuses to attend because he will not misrepresent belief, describing his epistemology as layered stories rather than certainty and himself as more mystic than materialist. Paglia's Sexual Personae gets sustained close reading and correction (he argues she omits Hermes, the liminal artist, and is a fujoshi who misunderstands male desire), and he treats aesthetic and literary justification as prior to argument. Persistent secondary threads: universities deserve destruction and cannot be reformed; utilitarian and EA reasoning is epistemically bankrupt (recurring "time man" parables, organ donation as institutionalized deceit); male versus female homosocial organization as the hidden variable behind political events; a contrarian lifestyle genre (travel is fake, recycling is fake, vegetables are neurotic ritual, preheating ovens is a scam); and open disgust at right-wing antisemitism and Nick Fuentes, whom he ranks with Chris-chan as an object of horror.

positions 1043 words

A self-described former neoliberal who says the Afghanistan/Iraq assumption that other peoples "were already American and just waiting for the opportunity to live like us" was the template error, and applies the same correction to European immigration: he uses "ethnostate" to mean a state organized around a shared cultural identity rather than biological race, and argues postwar European homogeneity was the artifact of nineteenth-century nationalism and wartime population transfers, not a natural baseline. His stated duties framework: equal obligations to all citizens regardless of immigrant status, the reciprocal duties of xenia to noncitizen immigrants, no special duties to nonimmigrants — and he rejects both "the wignat position and the open borders position" as denying the sacred obligation citizens owe one another. He demands that people who call America a creedal nation define the creed and say how it is tested and enforced, and expects no answer. He supports deporting noncitizens for speech on the grounds that free speech for citizens is worth more than admitting foreigners whose values are incompatible with a liberal society, and holds that a well-designed selective immigration system would be great but that America is manifestly incapable of implementing one. He is an avowed Anglo cultural supremacist, race-agnostic ("the Peoples of the Queue"), who treats the Royal Navy's and the Union's destruction of slavery — a near-universal human institution he catalogued caliphate by caliphate, Mongol by Comanche — as the greatest act of the Anglo world order, procured at deliberate strategic and national cost. He never voted for Trump, calls him a man of vulgar soul, senseless cruelty and low cunning who can make America great but never worthy, credits him only for being honest about his own debasement, and separately regards the threats against Denmark, Canada and the UK over Chagos as dishonorable and shameful; he votes for his father. He backed the Iran war on explicitly practical grounds — the IRI has been in low-grade war with the US for decades, the region can't be abandoned, and this was a generational opportunity while Iranian proxies were degraded — while calling the moral case for removing the revolution separate and secondary. On economics he insists positive economics is *silent* about the good, not agnostic, cites Hayek 1945 against Strauss's charge of technicians without vision, tells people to study public choice instead of the history of political philosophy, and reads the state-of-nature tradition as a groping toward agent models without math or Darwin. He is savage toward effective altruism and utilitarianism: long-run counterfactuals are unmeasurable so A/B-tested interventions are the drunk under the streetlight; the organ donor box exists to harvest organs without asking next of kin, which is lying; utilitarians presume to impose their preferences as objective reality while most people plainly have lexicographic preferences. He calls universities unreformable — 95% slop, unwilling to distinguish coal from gems, plus nondischargeable debt — and wants them made an object lesson, most post-1700 disciplines abandoned and the professoriate barred. He argues cultural Christianity can only be revived if elites actually attend, fund and lead churches and live exemplary lives, which he expects they will not do; he does not attend himself because he does not believe in the sense that would be required, describing his epistemology as layered stories, none of them the territory, his ethics as aesthetically and narratively justified, and himself as more mystic than materialist. He treats the sacred anthropologically (Frazer, Eliade) as containment of pure and impure rather than good and bad, and holds all religions have Kegan 2/3/4/5 variants. Against Paglia he argues the Apollonian/Dionysian pair needs a third, Hermetic term — liminal, alchemical, the artist's own role, embodied in Shakespeare and Prospero — and that Paglia is a fujoshi whose model of masculinity is derived from how gay men see male beauty and is therefore not generalizable. He dismisses Kant as unaesthetic and Rousseau as a personality disorder. On marriage he prescribes no Talks, no chore rotations, no fairness accounting: a shared life aesthetic, complete mutual trust in the other's good faith, continuous attention to the other's state, and "we are never getting divorced" as a commitment device eliminating brinkmanship. On parenting he wants kids given a foundational construction of an enchanted world before any deconstruction, opposes shoving adult psychodrama into children's media, maximizes their volition subject to prudence, and calls iPad-based public schooling blackpilling. He argues badgering people in fertility treatment to adopt is unjust and morally corrupt because adoptable older children are severely selected for difficulty and adoption is supererogatory caritas, not a market that money can clear. He states flatly that men do not appreciate high body counts, that this is not routable, and the injustice is telling young women it won't matter. He holds that a functional society would have Good Samaritan protections for bystanders who intervene physically, that intervention can't be made obligatory in a society that punishes it, but that no one should be proud of declining. He believes the optimal amount of ingroup bias varies by role and duty. He accepts IQ heritability while needling IQ truthers about height, and insists regression is to the mean of a reference population — plausibly the grandparents — not to 100. He has become deliberately brutal about policy: any complex policy will be ruthlessly exploited by parties with no commitment to common good, so he prefers crude ones; he says we are in a low-trust bad equilibrium and a distributed civil war, in a "calvinball era" of contested law comparable to Jackson or the New Deal, and that the judiciary has now burned its credibility the way the universities and media did. He thinks masked ICE agents are a symptom rather than a cause, that a society gets the police it deserves, and that given crumbled state capacity the only options are no police or brutal police. His forecast is explicit: geopolitical retreat of the American empire, an insoluble fertility crisis, a debt crisis, and cannibalized load-bearing institutions — four generational problems that will not all be solved, ending in something like the Crisis of the Third Century, with no recovery to the prior trajectory. He therefore advises building walled gardens, rolling your own institutions, tending your own garden, and looking out fiercely for the few people you can trust.

causal_models 562 words

Reasons like an economist who stopped trusting economics: the first move is always to ask what selection process produced the population being observed and what incentives the actors actually face, and the second is to deny that anyone is behaving irrationally — "irrational" means you have misidentified their objective. The adoption thread is the template: children available for older-child adoption are selected twice over, first by whatever destroyed their families and again by surviving repeated placement failures, so the observed difficulty is a filtering artifact rather than a random draw; the same move gets reused on white men leaving literary fiction (the career lost its juice, and men fleeing a field is the signal), on H1B wage differentials (cheapness is the point, not a side effect), on foster care, on elite admissions, on who becomes a leftist activist. Beneath incentives sits a slower, harder layer he treats as genuinely foundational: shared culture and descent. He uses Albion's Seed compulsively — Yankee, Butternut, Borderer, Cavalier — as a working ethnography of American politics, insists republics require Aristotelian *homonoia* and that welfare states dissolve when national identity is degraded because people won't fund an outgroup, and reaches for ancient population genetics (Yamnaya, WHG, the Hajnal line possibly downstream of soil) as real explanation rather than trivia. Institutions are load-bearing, expensive to build, cheap to hollow out, and effectively unrecoverable once trust breaks — path dependency, saeculum, "history has a unit root," a transmission failure between generations is the actual mechanism of civilizational death, and the Crisis of the Third Century is his working analogy for America. Because he thinks any policy will be immediately strip-mined by interested parties in a low-trust environment, he has abandoned technocracy for crude, hard-to-exploit rules; complex optimizing policy presupposes the high-trust system whose absence is the problem. Elite behavior propagates downward by mimicry, so cultural Christianity died not from metaphysics but from elites bodily leaving their pews and rerouting money to PACs and think tanks; NGOs displaced grassroots charity and thereby brought it under central political control. The military is his standing counterexample to bureaucratic rot: retained telos, real consequences for failure, permission to use IQ tests, hence competence — civil service decay is diagnosed as diffused responsibility plus survivable failure. Conflict escalates through ratchets and reciprocal defection; the awokening broke a postwar racial truce that cannot be renegotiated because no one will trust enforcement, and the resulting simmering civil war is self-perpetuating with no faction strong enough to impose orthodoxy. He credits Campbell's Law for wrecking empiricism, treats economics as storytelling with models that are useful and not real, dismisses EA's A/B-tested interventions as the drunk under the streetlight, and regards the information environment itself as a causal variable requiring "cogsec." Biology is upstream of politics more than he says outright: heritability of IQ and height, assortative mating changing the reference population for regression to the mean, women's political shifts attributed to hormonal contraceptives and pregnancy-induced neuroendocrine change, sexual preferences treated as embedded and unroutable. Aesthetics is not decoration but cause — statesmen who don't care about appearances don't care about winning, and his own ethics are justified narratively rather than argumentatively. Finally, he rejects "luck" as the frame for inheritance: what you have was procured by the deliberate labor and sacrifice of specific named ancestors, and Rawls's veil is a category error about where outcomes come from.

Certainty is inverted from the usual pattern: near-total confidence on aesthetic and literary judgment, on reading what a person is really doing, and on moral disgust — delivered as flat unqualified verdicts — while empirical and predictive claims come wrapped in "idk," "caveat emptor," "im just armchair speculating," "big idk," "who knows anything tho (really)," and explicit invitations to be rebutted by someone who actually knows. preoccupations 522 words

Civilizational decline dominates everything: he returns compulsively to the thesis that America and the West are in irreversible collapse, itemized as four interlocking crises — the fertility crash, sovereign debt, the retreat of the American empire before China, and the cannibalization of load-bearing institutions into a low-trust society — with a repeated insistence that no faction is competent or willing to fix any of it, that he expects to die in poverty, and that the correct response is to tend one's garden, build high-walled private institutions, and prepare children for a worse world.
