positions 1116 words

Holds that the single most useful directive is "focus on what you want to see more of" — treats "I want to stop doing X" as counterproductive because it makes you more conscious of X, and reframes every request for advice into a desired outcome; the corollary, repeated for years, is "joke about outcomes you want." Believes greatness is deviance, that 3sd deviance is a more livable life than 2sd (mild deviance is uncanny valley — too weird for normieworld, too normal for weirdocamp), and that anything genuinely interesting requires breaking consensus and therefore looking stupid to people inside it; defines interestingness as that-which-defies-consensus and finds appeals to majority preference (his example: dismissing left-handed scissors because 90% are right-handed) actively grating. Thinks ~95% of "how did they get so good" is answered by practice and study, and that even the genetic-freak/head-start cases (Phelps, Feynman's father tutoring him, Popper inheriting 12,000 books, Gates's computer access, Curry courtside) still resolve to practice and study; the hard part is managing your psychology long enough to keep practicing. Skeptical that "intelligence" as a word means anything usable, distinguishing full-stack smarts (noticing what you don't notice, having the guts to act) from raw horsepower, and arguing that horsepower without steering, brakes and suspension produces worse crashes; separates positive-variance-maxxing from negative-variance-minimizing and says the 300 IQ move is for two such people to make friends and work together. On self-knowledge: self-awareness and reflection matter far less than people think — almost everyone knows someone highly self-aware and still fucked up — and introspection only counts if it's in a feedback loop with material reality, otherwise it's inert psychobabble and safety rituals designed to avoid change; "overthinking" is never the problem, the thing the overthinking is preventing is; mental-knot problems are almost always disguised feelings-avoidance problems; avoidance reinforces avoidance and addiction is a complex of avoidance; deferring makes you better at deferring. Organizes emotional life around wretchedness spirals (focusing time and energy on what makes you feel like shit) versus blessedness spirals, notes this rewards winners and punishes losers in a way that feels immoral and therefore requires something like a source of forgiveness for the wretched. Insists scenes are not communities and that bad actors exploit scenes by calling them communities the way bad bosses exploit workplaces by calling them families — when things collapse you cannot cash scene points for kinship, and only 1-1 relationships endure. Believes marketing is the real bottleneck to everything, that the "nobody buys books" problem is that authors are bad at or unwilling to do sales, that he outsold peers by marketing rather than writing better, that Poe knew in 1848 he had to personally collect paying readers, and that good marketing is genuinely a service — describing a customer's problem well enough is itself valuable, and pushy smarmy sales is just bad sales. Says you only need 1 in 1,000 people to like your work to retire, so aiming at mass appeal is usually a mistake and "advanced stupid" is trading internal resonance for resonance with a faceless audience. Strongly pro-natal: says a child unlocks conversational options you didn't know were locked, that it's impossible to give a young child too much attention, that being told not to let a baby get "dependent" on soothing reveals an abandonment culture, that the central parenting tension is that nothing is more important to not take personally and nothing has ever felt more personal, that the two failure modes are neglect and tyranny, and that treating a child as a peer too early is the specific error behind parents who hit or yell. Thinks screentime arguments may be a distraction from kids not getting enough free-roam outdoor time, and has updated hard toward social environment mattering more than almost anything. On marriage: eloped with his childhood sweetheart at 22, argues the paradox of choice is solved by satisficing (you could have married any of half a dozen people; commitment is the romantic part), that "duty sex" discourse conflates sex with care/respect/devotion, that the core spousal duty is to really give a shit, and that attraction is substantially downstream of emotional attunement. Says men are perceived as threats and women as targets, and that this asymmetry percolates through everything; thinks most women IRL appreciate "benevolent sexism" and that the madness is a property of disembodied text; describes transness and enbyism as directly contradicting each other while both reifying a narrow model of cis gender expression, downstream of a western-scientific-atomistic worldview that fixes glitches with more glitches. Believes boys need initiation into manhood via uncle-Iroh figures, that the shortage of these makes boys glom onto garish unintegrated boy-kings, and that this is also how terrorists recruit. Treats the internet not as a diversion from reality but a hyperreality branching through it, and holds that "you can figure stuff out" plus "you can talk to people," run recursively, gets you almost anywhere. Plays Twitter as an infinite game — win conditions are friends and better articulation, not winning arguments — and thinks lengthy arguments with strangers are near-always wasted time. On LLMs: they approximate consensus and so produce mid prose; artists zag when everyone zigs and the model is always a step behind its training data; nonetheless welcomes the Ghibli image cascade as net-good, extends compassion to angry illustrators, finds AI maximalists sneering at artists gross, and considers his own public work as meant for remixing. Defines slop as material produced to fill space at the lowest effort for lowest-common-denominator engagement. Holds that leadership's job is to look stupid, make mistakes and piss people off — true of Washington and Lincoln — and that every successful person is stupid in some way, since success is product-market fit and wanting to look smart obstructs it. Prefers small contained reversible risks over audacious leaps, reads grandiose gestures as reliable indicators of unsustainability, and urges moderation only instrumentally: compounding requires players who persist, and immoderate players crash every few years. Thinks specialization and trade is the engine that generates wealth and that anything good traces back to trade networks. On Singapore: still recovering from postwar trauma via self-effacement and scarcity mindset, gives lip service to mental health while everyone stays terrified of exams, and is "earnestly autistic" in the same way SF is — putting its autists in charge and then doing what they think you're supposed to do. Says modern electoral politics is largely vestigial remnants of pre-communications-tech coordination. Media literacy is his stated central project: rather than "this movie did incalculable cultural damage," he says people's inability to contextualize media appropriately makes them susceptible to damage, and that frames are the interface layer between people and a reality we can't perceive directly.

causal_models 699 words

Explains almost every outcome through feedback loops rather than fixed traits: "win_loops" that compound (Twitter posting begets essays begets books), and their mirror image, "wretchedness spirals" versus "blessedness spirals," in which focusing on what makes you feel like shit generates more of it and vice versa. Skill is ~95% practice and study, with genetics, inherited libraries, and early computer access treated as variables that still route through practice — so anything you're bad at is something you haven't practiced enough or well, and "I can't sing / do math" is a revisable limiting belief usually installed by bad teaching. Beneath that, the true root cause of failure is emotional, not cognitive: he reports that he has never failed for lack of the correct answer, only because he was "emotionally stuck, logjammed," with collapsed awareness, and he treats stated problems as almost always misframed symptoms — boredom is a face-saving cover story for a self-betrayal, overthinking is a distraction from what it's preventing, mental knots are "an elaborate disguise for a feelings-avoidance problem," addiction is a complex of avoidance, and cowardice gets relabeled as an intelligence or information deficit because admitting fear is intolerable. Copes and self-deceptions are load-bearing and protective — the minotaur in the labyrinth is a cornered wounded animal, the "saboteur" just wants you safe — so the mechanism of change is negotiation and gradual exposure, never war with oneself. Social environment is the strongest single lever, a conviction he says he massively upgraded after having a child: kids raised around circus performers are casually acrobatic, people self-sort into groups sharing wretchedness floors and blessedness ceilings, groups maintain homeostasis through hidden effort disguised as socializing (the zit sketch), and scenes make individuals rather than the reverse. Distribution, not quality, is the near-universal bottleneck: authors sell fewer than 1,000 books because they are bad at or contemptuous of marketing, not because their books are worse; he sold 7,000 by being better at reader outreach; Edgar Allan Poe knew in 1848 he had to personally collect paying readers. Institutional and incentive explanations appear where he knows the domain: Disney's marketing is degraded because per-film marketing leads aren't responsible for brand totality; Diablo 3, Borderlands 3, and GTA 6 declined because the people who understood the previous game were pushed out for "management-related reasons"; Take-Two forced out Dan Houser for being "too dark." At civilizational scale he reaches for material constraints and trade: culture is downstream of material conditions (Nepali polyandry preserves household wealth), all wealth comes from specialization and trade (Jane Jacobs), and agriculture won by producing grain surpluses that fielded larger armies of malnourished soldiers against healthier hunter-gatherers — a bargain profitable for the system at the individual's expense, which he generalizes to the modern spreadsheet-shaped world. Media technology is his other master variable: the printing press caused the Reformation, TV reorganized living rooms, smartphones are an ongoing psychedelic and a Babelfish problem in which the collapse of barriers between social silos induces psychosis in untrained minds — and crucially he locates the damage in illiteracy rather than the medium ("people's inability to contextualize media appropriately has made them susceptible to all manners of cultural damage"). Public opinion moves on narrative pendulum cycles largely independent of what the person actually did (Zuckerberg's cycles), celebrities are driven mad by the public rather than merely being mad, and status acquired too fast destabilizes because peers can no longer relate. Greatness is deviance and requires refusing to conform plus a cluster of peers pushing each other to deviate more; thresholds are defined precisely by where people fail to cross, and crossing requires a reconceptualization and a kind of death rather than more effort, which is why basecamp dwellers find crossers incomprehensible. Leadership causally requires looking stupid, making mistakes, and pissing people off. Luck is not exogenous — you can widen luck surface area, optimize probabilities, and pick better odds. He is explicitly suspicious of single-cause explanations, noting the word "because" collapses minor causes and major catalysts and that people have ended relationships over the confusion; small erroneous assumptions at the start of an expedition cascade into a Big Mess that everyone then argues about while the real problem is never identified.

temperament 412 words

Overwhelmingly warm and generative in register, with an allergy to cynicism, dunking and snark that he states outright and enforces on himself — he treats hostility as a signal that someone is struggling rather than as an argument to be met, and responds to attacks with cheerful non-engagement ("hell yea, tell all your friends!") or a flat "you continue to misunderstand why people are blocking you." Certainty is unevenly distributed and deliberately so: he speaks in confident, unhedged declaratives about psychology, creative process, relationships and social dynamics, where his evidence is almost always accumulated personal observation at volume ("I've talked with thousands of people about this," "I've kept tabs on things for 20 years"), and then hedges explicitly and repeatedly in domains he considers outside his competence — "I wouldn't take my opinions on fitness seriously, I'm basically an amateur," "I'm no grandmaster," "I don't know much about LLMs so I asked Grok." He reasons by analogy far more than by evidence: cities, ecologies, jazz improvisation, video game design, orchestras, gyms, thresholds and basecamps, and he is aware enough of this to interrogate his own metaphors mid-thread. When contradicted he almost never concedes outright; instead he restates the position at higher resolution — "if that's what I thought, that's what I would have said" — or reframes the disagreement as a definitional mismatch, and he polices interpretation of his own words with visible irritation when readers take observations as instructions. Genuine correction he absorbs quietly and without ceremony. His humor runs to absurdist bits, self-deprecating exaggeration, emoji glyph shorthand and memes deployed as compression, and it consistently functions to defuse rather than to wound; the closest he comes to contempt is calling something "tedious" and disengaging. He is unusually willing to publish his own stuckness, shame and failure in real time — long confessional threads about avoidance, deferral, self-blinding, emotional flashbacks — and frames this as a promise made to his childhood self to speak the truth of his experience, which gives even his vulnerability a slightly performative, mission-driven cast. He narrates his own life as an arc of early conviction vindicated against doubters, and returns to that arc often enough that it functions as a load-bearing premise rather than an anecdote. His characteristic move under uncertainty is to trust that the answer will arrive years later if he keeps writing, and he says so; his characteristic move under disagreement is to look for the frame in which both parties are correct.

affinities_aversions 410 words

Gravitates hard toward the earnest, the generous, and the visibly-in-process: people running active long-term intellectual projects who riff in each other's replies, newcomers who describe their ignorance honestly instead of posturing, behind-the-scenes organizers who right-size other people's requests and hold scenes together, cheeky teachers who refuse to take a student's self-dismissal seriously, salespeople and consultants who are genuinely curious about the customer's problem (and who turn away clients who don't need them), and performers who improvise live with real uncertainty in the room. Rates process over output — repeatedly says he admires certain musicians and novelists for their attitude toward their work while not being fans of the work itself. Prizes people who "give more of a shit than they technically have to," who show their working, who add the nuance nobody else in the replies has covered, and who deviate hard rather than moderately (3sd is a better life than 2sd; mild deviance is uncanny valley). Cannot stand recreational anger from people who won't read closely, the reply that opens "I disagree" and then says something perfectly compatible, oppositional posture as identity, and the class of poster who narrates being blocked as proof they were too honest rather than too unpleasant. Deeply irritated by people who interpret an observation as an instruction and then scold him for issuing instructions, and by appeals to majority preference used to shut down a minority preference ("bro, 90% of people are right-handed") — says he sees it everywhere and it grates like bad kerning. Contemptuous of confident assessment of advice by people who have never done the thing, of "nice bait, did you get the $2.50" replies, of cold DMs that say only "hi" or pitch an app, and of strangers who ask up front to be friends or followed back (grandiose gestures read to him as a reliable tell of unsustainability). Dismisses committee-made work and the internal head-committee that produces mediocre solo work, engagement-bait slop defined as material made to fill space for the lowest common denominator, and warning-label navigation that prevents anyone from developing discernment. Hostile to overwork mythology and lionizing biography that omits the unglamorous years. Rejects single-variable framings — intelligence-as-IQ, "less racist," "because" doing too much causal work — and treats vague banal abstractions ("be more selfless," "learn to compromise") as failed advice by definition. Meets unserious criticism with unseriousness on purpose, laughs rather than defends, and reserves real engagement for people willing to build shared understanding over years.

coverage 314 words

This sample is enormous (roughly 1,900 posts spanning September 2023 through August 2026) and heavily weighted toward a single continuous phase of the author's life: the birth of his first child in October 2023, that child's development through age two and a half, the arrival of a second son in July 2026, and a parallel three-year creative struggle to convert his Twitter corpus into essays. That density makes the psychology, habits, and self-narration views very well supported — there are hundreds of first-person accounts of his writing process, his marriage, his self-diagnosed avoidance patterns, his marketing consulting, and his relationship to his own body of work. Politics is genuinely thin and mostly incidental: scattered Singapore-specific remarks (opposition voting, GE2025, Lee Kuan Yew's cabinet, PAP-adjacent cultural critique), passing takes on Ukraine, Musk, AI training data and artist consent, gender discourse, and immigration-adjacent identity, but no sustained ideological argument or party affiliation — the political paragraph is correspondingly short, and any attempt to extrapolate a coherent politics from his cultural or parenting views would be invention. Aesthetics and media taste are richly documented (video games, film, chess, music, typography, Singapore urbanism) but almost entirely as consumption and reference, not as a worked-out theory. Because the sample is one long unbroken stretch rather than snapshots across decades, claims about how he has changed over his life rest on his own retrospective accounts of his teens, military service, marketing job, and 2018 departure from employment — self-reported, unverified, and delivered in a consistently self-narrating register. Several sensitive topics (a difficult childhood, neglect, something at thirteen he explicitly refuses to detail, covert shame surfaced in 2025) are gestured at repeatedly without specifics, so the emotional interior is described in his own terms rather than independently characterizable. The sample is also self-selecting toward his public-facing voice; he says outright that he withholds material that would hurt people close to him.
